No monthly fees. No platform-held payouts.

Latuos, pronounced lah-too-ohs, is a digital product delivery
platform that charges a 2% platform fee per sale with no monthly
fees. Stripe processing fees apply separately. Sellers connect
their own Stripe account, remain merchant of record, and receive
payments directly. Latuos handles checkout and file delivery. It
is operated by Organo Solutions Ltd, a company registered in
England and Wales.

How does the Stripe-first model work?

Latuos handles storefront and delivery. Stripe handles
payments. Buyers pay your connected Stripe account directly.

Your business runs payments through its own Stripe account.
Latuos does not hold seller balances and does not run a
platform payout queue.

What you keep

Direct Stripe relationship and payout visibility

Refunds and payment operations are handled in your Stripe
account

A portable setup if you change delivery tools later

Who Latuos is best for

Latuos works best for creators who already bring some of their
own traffic and want a simple storefront without giving up
payment ownership. That includes sellers with ebooks,
templates, printables, guides, digital art, and small bundle
catalogs who care more about low overhead and payout clarity
than marketplace discovery.

It is a weaker fit if you want a built-in marketplace to drive
most sales, if you need a large app ecosystem before
launching, or if you prefer the platform to hold more of the
payment workflow for you. Latuos is intentionally narrower
than that. It focuses on direct sales with a connected Stripe
account rather than acting like an operator between you and
your customer revenue.

That distinction matters operationally. When Stripe belongs to
the seller, payout timing, refunds, disputes, and reporting
stay visible in the seller's own account instead of being
abstracted behind a marketplace balance. That makes it easier
to understand cash flow, change storefront tools later, and
keep the payment relationship attached to the business rather
than to a single platform.

See how it works

Connect your Stripe account, upload a digital product, and start
selling in minutes.

Stripe-first by design

Latuos handles delivery and storefront operations. Stripe
handles payment processing and payouts. Funds flow directly
from buyer to your connected Stripe account.

Included in the 2% platform fee

The 2% refers to the Latuos platform fee only. Stripe
processing is separate. VAT may apply where required.

Checkout and digital delivery workflow

Secure file access and post-purchase delivery

Seller control with no platform-held balances

When do you actually get paid?

Latuos does not hold a seller balance or run its own payout
queue, so it does not add a separate waiting period. Buyer
payments are processed through your connected Stripe account,
and Stripe sends available funds to your bank according to your
Stripe payout schedule. For US Stripe accounts, Stripe lists a
standard T+2 settlement timing, meaning funds generally become
available for payout about 2 business days after the payment is
captured. This is separate from the time required for a payout
to reach your bank.

Actual timing also depends on whether it is your first payout,
your country, payment method, industry and risk profile,
account status, payout schedule, and bank processing times.
Stripe says the first live payout is typically scheduled 7–14
days after the first successful payment and may take longer in
some cases.

For eligible Stripe Dashboard users, Instant Payouts typically
settle in an eligible bank account or debit card within 30
minutes. Stripe lists a 1.5% fee for US, Australian, and New
Zealand accounts and a 1% fee for Canadian, UK, and
Singaporean accounts. Eligibility, an instant-available
balance, a supported payout method, per-transaction minimum
and maximum amounts, and daily limits apply.

More detail on payout timing, eligibility and verification, and
how refunds and disputes can affect available funds is covered
in the
instant payouts guide .

Latuos pricing, payout, and tax questions

Does Latuos support Apple Pay and Klarna?

Latuos uses Stripe Checkout, so Apple Pay and Klarna can
appear when they are available for the seller's Stripe
account, customer location, currency, and payment method
setup.

Does Latuos deliver files after purchase?

Yes. Latuos handles checkout and secure file delivery for
digital products after purchase.

What can I sell on Latuos?

Latuos is built for direct digital products such as
downloads, templates, ebooks, guides, printables, digital
art, tutorial files, and small downloadable product
catalogs.

Does Latuos hold seller payouts?

No. Latuos is built around direct Stripe payouts. Customer
payments go to the seller's connected Stripe account, and
Latuos does not run a platform payout queue.

Who is merchant of record on Latuos?

The seller is merchant of record because the buyer pays the
seller's connected Stripe account. Latuos provides the
storefront, checkout, delivery, and product-management
layer.

Who files VAT and sales tax on my sales?

You do, because you are merchant of record and buyers pay
your own Stripe account, the same as selling from your own
website. Stripe Tax can calculate and collect tax at
checkout depending on your setup. Merchant-of-record
platforms bundle tax filing into their higher fees; on
Latuos you keep the direct Stripe relationship and handle
compliance yourself. See
how taxes change when you switch stacks .

How fast do payouts arrive?

Payouts follow your own Stripe account's payout schedule,
which you control from your Stripe dashboard. On Stripe's
standard schedule that is typically within a few business
days of the sale. There is no Latuos approval step or
platform payout queue in between. More detail in
who controls payouts on digital product sales .

Who handles refunds and chargebacks?

You issue refunds from your own Stripe dashboard, and
disputes and chargebacks go through Stripe directly to you
because the buyer paid your Stripe account. Latuos does not
hold a balance that can be frozen while a dispute runs. The
full model is covered in
Stripe account ownership explained .

Does Latuos support subscriptions or memberships?

Not today. Latuos is built for one-time sales of digital
downloads such as templates, ebooks, guides, and printables.
If subscriptions or memberships are core to your business,
Latuos is not the right fit yet.

What the 2% fee is paying for

The 2% platform fee covers the hosted storefront, checkout flow,
secure file delivery, and the product layer that sits on top of
your Stripe account. It pays for the software that helps a
seller publish products and deliver them after purchase, not for
payment processing itself.

Stripe still handles card processing, payout timing, refunds,
disputes, and the payment dashboard because the seller keeps the
direct Stripe relationship. That split is intentional. It lets a
seller use a hosted storefront while keeping payment visibility
in the system that actually processes the money.

As volume grows, the economics become easier to compare. A
percentage fee is usually attractive when a seller is testing,
launching, or selling inconsistently because there is no fixed
monthly overhead. Once revenue is stable, the break-even
question becomes whether a subscription platform with 0%
platform fees would cost less overall. The fee structure stays
simple, but the right choice depends on how predictable your
sales volume is.

Where seller onboarding is available

Latuos sellers can sell to customers worldwide using their own
Stripe account.

To connect Stripe on Latuos today, your Stripe account must be
for a business or individual seller based in one of these
supported countries:

United States

Canada

Australia

New Zealand

Singapore

If your business or individual seller account is based in one of
these countries, you can use Latuos to sell worldwide with
direct Stripe payouts.

content:
About Latuos

About Latuos: selling digital products with Stripe

Quick answer

Latuos, pronounced lah-too-ohs and spelled L-A-T-U-O-S, is a
digital product sales platform that connects to a seller's
own Stripe account. Creators can upload a product, publish a
storefront, accept payment, and deliver files automatically
without waiting for a marketplace payout. Latuos has no
monthly fee and charges a 2% platform fee on each completed
sale. Stripe processing fees are separate, and VAT may apply
to Latuos fees where required.

Payments go directly to the seller's connected Stripe
account. The seller remains merchant of record, sees the
payment in Stripe, manages refunds and disputes, and sees
balances and payout timing through that account. Latuos
provides the storefront, checkout connection, and secure
delivery layer, but it does not hold seller balances or
provide marketplace discovery. It is built for creators who
bring their own audience and sell downloads, templates,
ebooks, digital art, printables, guides, Notion links, or
small bundles.

From setup to delivery

How does Latuos work for selling digital products?

Latuos keeps the storefront, checkout connection, and delivery
tools in one workflow while payments, refunds, disputes,
reporting, and payout timing remain in the seller's own Stripe
account.

01

Check seller eligibility

Seller onboarding is available for businesses or individuals
based in the United States, Canada, the United Kingdom,
Australia, New Zealand, and Singapore. Supported sellers can
sell to customers worldwide, subject to Stripe availability,
restricted-business rules, sanctions, and applicable legal
requirements.

02

Connect Stripe

Connect a Stripe account through Stripe Connect Standard.
The seller remains merchant of record, and the payment
relationship stays tied to the seller's Stripe account.

03

Set up the product

Add downloadable files, links such as Notion URLs, or both.
Set the price, currency, product images, description, and
product-specific download limits before publishing.

04

Publish and share

Publish the product or storefront and share its public URL
with an existing audience. Latuos is not a marketplace, so
sellers bring their own traffic.

05

Buyer pays through Stripe

Checkout runs through Stripe. The customer payment goes to
the seller's connected Stripe account, and Latuos collects
its 2% platform fee through the Stripe connection.

06

Deliver and manage the sale

Latuos sends purchase confirmation and secure product
access. The seller sees charges, balances, payout timing,
refunds, and disputes in Stripe.

What Latuos handles

Latuos provides hosted storefronts, product pages, checkout
connection, secure file or link delivery, buyer re-download
access, customer lists, basic analytics, discount codes, and
optional thank-you messages.

What Stripe and the seller handle

Stripe processes the payment and controls the seller's
payout schedule. The seller handles refunds, disputes,
chargebacks, payment reporting, and tax compliance through
their own Stripe account and business records.

What can you sell with Latuos?

Latuos is designed for downloadable and link-based digital
products sold directly to an audience.

Downloads

Templates

Ebooks

Digital art

Printables

Guides

Notion links

Small bundles

Each product can include up to 10 files, up to 500 MB per file
and 2 GB total per product. Sellers can set download limits,
let buyers re-download purchased files and links, and use
optional PDF stamping with the buyer email and transaction ID
for PDF products.

Simple pricing

How much does Latuos cost?

No monthly fee. Latuos charges 2% per completed sale.

Stripe processing is charged separately. VAT may apply to the
Latuos platform fee where required.

See full pricing

Clear answers

Frequently asked questions

Is there a monthly fee for Latuos?

No. Latuos has no monthly subscription. Sellers pay the 2%
Latuos platform fee only when a sale is completed.

How do Latuos fees work?

Latuos charges 2% of each completed sale. Stripe charges its
payment-processing fee separately, and VAT may apply to the
Latuos platform fee where required. Latuos does not charge a
monthly fee or hold the seller's balance.

Where is seller onboarding available?

Seller onboarding is available for businesses or individuals
based in the United States, Canada, the United Kingdom,
Australia, New Zealand, and Singapore. Supported sellers can
sell to customers worldwide through their own connected
Stripe account, subject to Stripe availability,
restricted-business rules, sanctions, and applicable tax or
legal requirements.

How does Latuos deliver digital products?

Latuos can deliver downloadable files, links such as Notion
URLs, or both after a successful Stripe payment. Each
product can include up to 10 files, up to 500 MB per file
and 2 GB total per product, with product-specific download
limits and buyer re-download access.

Is Latuos different from Lemon Squeezy, Gumroad, or Payhip?

Yes. Latuos is built around the seller's own Stripe account:
the seller remains merchant of record and payments go to
that connected account. Gumroad and Lemon Squeezy use a
merchant-of-record model. Payhip can connect to seller
payment processors and offers tiered plans. These are
different operating models, so the right fit depends on
whether a seller prioritizes direct payment ownership or
wants a platform to take on more transaction administration.

See the maintained comparisons for
Latuos vs Gumroad ,
Latuos vs Lemon Squeezy , and
Latuos vs Payhip . Those pages cite the companies' official public pricing
and help sources.

When do I get paid?

Payments go directly to the seller's connected Stripe
account, so payouts follow that Stripe account's own payout
schedule. Latuos does not hold the seller's sales balance or
add a separate payout queue on top of Stripe.

How are refunds and disputes handled?

Refunds, disputes, and chargebacks are handled through the
seller's own Stripe account because the seller remains
merchant of record. Latuos provides the storefront and
delivery layer and does not hold or queue the payment
between the seller and Stripe.

Latuos exists for creators who want the storefront and
delivery layer without giving up their direct Stripe
relationship. The product is designed around one core idea:
sellers should keep payment-account ownership and payout
visibility in their own Stripe setup instead of relying on a
platform-held balance.

What fee assumptions does the calculator use?

It uses Latuos at 2% plus Stripe processing, Gumroad
direct/profile sales at 10% + $0.50 plus processing, Etsy at
6.5% + $0.20 plus US Etsy Payments processing, Payhip Free
at 5% plus processing, and Notion Marketplace at 10% + $0.40
with processing included.

Does Latuos include Stripe processing in its 2% platform
fee?

No. Latuos charges a 2% platform fee, and Stripe processing
is estimated separately. The Stripe assumption used here is
2.9% + $0.30 for US online card processing.

Are calculator results exact bills?

No. Results are planning estimates based on public pricing
assumptions. Actual fees can vary by country, currency,
payment method, plan, taxes, ads, reserves, optional
services, and platform-specific rules.

on public USD fee information checked in June 2026. Processing
assumptions vary by country and payment method. Actual costs may
vary by plan, country, payment method, tax settings, optional
features, and promotions. Assumptions: Gumroad direct/profile
sales (not Discover) with card processing charged separately;
Etsy digital downloads with Etsy Payments and one $0.20 listing
renewal per sale, using a common US processing assumption;
Payhip Free plan; and Notion Marketplace fees (10% + $0.40) with
payment processing included. Notion may also apply a 1% FX fee
for creators outside the US, and Marketplace payouts follow
Notion's payout schedule and eligibility rules. Etsy estimates
exclude setup fees, regulatory operating fees, deposit fees,
Offsite Ads, on-site ads, and any currency conversion fees where
applicable. Calculations use common pricing configurations.
Actual fees vary by payment method, region, and optional
features. Always verify current fees directly on each official
pricing page before making decisions. Whop is not included here
because its pricing can vary by payment method and enabled
payment features. Latuos is an independent product and is not
affiliated with, sponsored by, or endorsed by Gumroad, Etsy,

Gumroad alternative for digital creators

Gumroad vs Latuos: Lower Fees or Marketplace Convenience?

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

Choose Gumroad if marketplace discovery and a
merchant-of-record setup matter more than fee savings.
Choose Latuos if you already bring your own audience and
want lower fees, payments to your own Stripe account, and
control of the payment account.

Gumroad is still one of the most visible platforms in
creator recommendations, and that matters if you want
Discover exposure or prefer the platform to sit between you
and the transaction. Latuos is a better fit when you care
more about margin, payout control, and owning the Stripe
account connected to your sales.

If you are searching Gumroad pricing, this page is the
side-by-side view: Gumroad pricing starts at 10% plus $0.50
on direct sales, while Latuos pricing is a 2% platform fee
plus Stripe processing.

This is not a question of which platform is better in every
case. Gumroad offers marketplace discovery and
merchant-of-record convenience. Latuos offers lower fees and
uses your own Stripe account. The right choice depends on
where your buyers come from and how much control you want
over payments. If you still need the broader shortlist
first, start with
Best Gumroad Alternatives in 2026 .

Pricing Model

Seller Payout Control

that may apply to Latuos platform fees for some sellers.

US pricing sources used for this comparison

Gumroad pricing

Payhip pricing

Etsy fee basics
and
Etsy US payment processing fees

Gumroad Pricing Versus Latuos Math

On a $29 direct sale, Gumroad takes $3.40 before any other
payment costs. Latuos takes a $0.58 platform fee, and
standard US Stripe online card processing adds about
$1.14, bringing the total to about $1.72. That leaves a
gap of about $1.68 per sale in Latuos's favor on the same
price point.

The difference gets larger as volume grows. At 50 direct
sales a month, that example is roughly $84 a month or
about $1,008 a year. Gumroad Discover can cost materially
more again because its marketplace rate can go as high as
30%.

Payhip Free is a useful middle reference point at 5%
before processing. Etsy is a different model because it
layers listing fees, transaction fees, and payment
processing on top of marketplace distribution. The
decision is not just which number is lowest. It is which
fee model fits the way you acquire customers.

The Real Structural Difference

The biggest difference is not just 10% plus $0.50 versus
2%. Gumroad acts as merchant of record and controls the
payout flow, while Latuos routes the sale to the seller's
own Stripe account. That changes both margin and
dependency.

Where Gumroad Still Wins

Gumroad still wins when built-in discovery is part of the
business model. If marketplace exposure matters more than
keeping a higher share of each sale, Gumroad is easier to
justify. It is also simpler for sellers who want the
platform to own more of the transaction flow instead of
connecting their own payment account.

That does not make Gumroad the better choice for every
seller. It means Gumroad is stronger when convenience and
distribution are the priority, while Latuos is stronger
when margin and payout ownership are the priority.

Best For

Latuos is best for creators selling digital products
directly to their own audience, especially when products
are lower priced or sold repeatedly enough that per-sale
fee drag compounds quickly.

Gumroad is best for creators who want a known platform
with marketplace exposure and are comfortable trading
margin and payout control for that distribution and
convenience.

Not For

Latuos is not the better choice if you need built-in
marketplace discovery or you do not want to connect and
manage your own Stripe account.

Gumroad is not the better choice if your traffic is
already yours and you mainly want to reduce fees, keep
payouts in your own Stripe dashboard, and avoid platform
control over the balance.

Frequently Asked Questions

Does Gumroad Charge 10% Plus $0.50 on Direct Sales?

Yes. Gumroad's published direct-sale pricing is 10% plus
$0.50 per transaction, so anyone researching Gumroad
pricing for direct sales should treat that as the
starting point before separate processing. Discover
sales can be charged at a higher marketplace rate.

Is Gumroad Merchant of Record?

Yes. Gumroad is merchant of record, which means Gumroad
controls the transaction flow and payout schedule rather
than the seller's own Stripe account.

Does Latuos Have a Discovery Marketplace?

No. Latuos is not a marketplace. It is built for
creators who already bring their own traffic and want
lower fees and payments to their own Stripe account.

When Does Latuos Usually Cost Less Than Gumroad?

For many direct sales, Latuos costs less because the
platform fee is 2% instead of Gumroad's 10% plus $0.50,
and the payment layer stays in the seller's own Stripe
account.

Payhip Fees vs Latuos: Free, Plus, and Pro Compared

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

Payhip is credible because it lets sellers use their own
payment processors. The tradeoff is that the Free plan is
still 5% plus processing, while the lower-fee plans add a
monthly subscription.

If you are searching for Payhip fees, the current pricing is
simple: Free is 5%, Plus is $29/month + 2%, and Pro is
$99/month + 0%, with payment processing still charged by the
processor. Latuos stays at a 2% platform fee with no monthly
subscription.

Choose Payhip if you need PayPal, Paystack, or a plan that
can go to 0% transaction fees at higher subscription tiers.
Choose Latuos if you want no monthly fee, a 2% platform fee
from day one, and a storefront built around direct Stripe
ownership.

Payhip Fee Math and Break-Even Points

On a $29 sale, Payhip Free costs $1.45 before processing.
Latuos costs $0.58 before processing. On a $100 sale,
Payhip Free costs $5 and Latuos costs $2. That makes
Payhip Free the more expensive option at everyday direct-
sale price points.

The break-even points matter once you look at the paid
tiers. Before payment processing, Payhip Plus does not
beat Latuos on platform-fee math: both charge 2% per sale,
and Payhip Plus adds $29/month on top. Payhip Pro beats
Latuos at about $4,950 in monthly sales because its $99
fixed cost becomes cheaper than 2%.

Processor rates still vary by country, so use the fee
calculator to compare your actual price point. But on
platform fees alone, that is the cleanest answer to the
"Payhip vs Latuos" question.

Where Payhip and Latuos Actually Differ

This comparison is not mainly about whether both support
Stripe. It is about fee model and product philosophy.
Payhip gives more plan choices and broader payment
options. Latuos stays simpler and cheaper at the point of
sale.

The real decision is broader payment options versus lower
ongoing cost.

Where the Real Decision Sits

If you need only Stripe and want the lowest fixed
commitment, Latuos is usually the simpler answer.

Refunds and disputes matter here too. When a platform sits
between the buyer and the seller, refund handling and
dispute flow are tied more closely to that platform's
rules and payout timing. If you want the cleanest setup
for direct Stripe ownership, that practical issue matters
as much as the headline fee.

Best for

Sellers who want a lower-fee direct-sales setup and do not
need PayPal as a payment method.

Not the best fit for

Sellers who need PayPal, Paystack, or who know a
subscription plan with lower platform percentage is better
for their volume.

your monthly revenue and average order value.

Quick Answer

Stan Store and Latuos both support digital selling, but the
bigger difference is pricing structure and setup. Latuos
uses a seller-connected Stripe flow, while Stan Store uses a
different payment model.

Choose Stan Store if you want an all-in-one creator
subscription stack and do not mind paying $29 a month before
you sell. Choose Latuos if you want no monthly fee, a
lower-risk start, and a storefront focused on digital
product checkout and using your own Stripe account.

or whether Stan Store takes a percentage of sales, the core
comparison is fixed monthly software cost versus Latuos's
percentage-based pricing.

Stan Store is built around creator brands, subscriptions,
and social audiences. Latuos is built around digital product
delivery with the lowest possible platform overhead. They
solve different problems, and comparing them mostly comes
down to whether you need a link-in-bio funnel or a
standalone storefront with a Stripe-connected payout flow.

SamCart pricing in 2026: what it costs and how Latuos compares

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

SamCart now markets one plan with all features, starting at
$79 per month and scaling upward as business revenue grows.
Latuos starts at $0 monthly and charges a 2% platform fee
plus Stripe processing, which is often simpler for direct
digital sellers who do not want a recurring software bill.

If you are searching for SamCart pricing, the most important
update is that SamCart is no longer framed around a static
public tier grid. Its current pricing page says all features
start at $79 per month and the price increases as your
business grows.

That changes the comparison. This is not a clean
multi-plan-versus-multi-plan page. It is a question of
whether a revenue-scaled SamCart subscription plus processor
fees makes more sense for you than Latuos's no-monthly-fee
2% model.

SamCart may fit better if you want broader checkout
optimization, subscriptions, and upsell tooling and you are
comfortable paying software fees every month. Latuos may be
simpler if you want a straightforward storefront for
one-time digital delivery with payments tied directly to
your own Stripe account.

Important Note on Interpretation

SamCart's June 2026 pricing page says pricing starts at
$79 per month and scales as your business grows. Because
that is not one universal flat rate, the math on this page
uses the published starting price as a directional
reference point rather than a guaranteed long-term rate
for every seller.

seller setup. Latuos fee examples on this page exclude any
VAT that may apply to Latuos platform fees for some
sellers.

How SamCart Pricing Works Now

SamCart's current pricing page says every feature is
available from day one and that pricing starts at $79 per
month. It also says that price adjusts as your business
grows, which means the sticker price is partly tied to
revenue rather than remaining one static public monthly
plan forever.

That is the key reason old SamCart comparisons age badly.
If you still see Launch, Grow, or Scale-style SamCart
pricing on a blog post, treat it as historical context,
not the current public offer.

What the $79 Starting Price Excludes

The $79 starting point is SamCart's software price, not a
fully loaded transaction cost. Payment processing still
sits on top. Official SamCart pricing and help materials
currently point to Stripe and PayPal integrations, and
SamCart also markets SamPay, its Stripe-powered payment
option, so the total cost depends on both the software
subscription and the processor path you choose.

In practice, that means SamCart may look cheaper than a
percentage fee at higher sales volume while still costing
more than a no-monthly-fee setup when revenue is low or
uneven. Because SamCart says pricing scales with growth,
the effective cost is not captured by the $79 headline
alone.

Break-Even Lens Using the Starting Price

If you compare SamCart's published $79 monthly starting
price against Latuos's 2% platform fee, the crossover
point is about $3,950 in monthly gross sales because 2% of
that amount is roughly $79.

Below that level, Latuos is usually lower on platform fees
alone. Above that level, SamCart's starting subscription
can look cheaper on platform fee alone. But that is not a
universal break-even because SamCart says its own price
scales upward as revenue grows.

The product model matters too. SamCart is a broader
checkout and funnel tool. Latuos is a simpler digital
product storefront with hosted checkout and delivery tied
directly to your own Stripe account. Similar monthly cost
does not mean the two platforms solve the same job.

When SamCart Makes Sense

SamCart may make more sense when your business already
supports a monthly software bill and you want more
conversion tooling around upsells, subscriptions, order
bumps, and checkout experimentation.

It is also a better fit when you evaluate software as part
of a higher-volume funnel stack rather than as the
lightest way to sell a digital download.

When Latuos Is the Simpler Fit

Latuos may be the simpler fit when you want to sell
one-time digital products, avoid a monthly platform bill,
and keep payments tied directly to your own Stripe account
instead of layering a revenue-scaled SaaS subscription on
top.

That is especially useful when you are early, have uneven
sales volume, or care more about payout visibility and
straightforward checkout-plus-delivery than about a broad
funnel stack.

SamCart's public pricing page says pricing starts at $79

currently presents one plan with all features rather
than old fixed public tiers.

Does SamCart still have multiple fixed plans?

Based on SamCart's June 2026 pricing page, it is not
marketing the old fixed Launch, Grow, or Scale-style
public plan grid. It now frames pricing around one plan
with all features and a price that adjusts with business
revenue.

Does SamCart's $79 starting price include payment

No. SamCart's software price is separate from payment
processing. Official SamCart pricing and help materials
currently point to Stripe and PayPal integrations, and
SamCart also markets SamPay, its Stripe-powered payment
option.

Latuos is usually cheaper on platform fees below about
$3,950 in monthly gross sales because 2% of that amount
is roughly $79. It is also simpler when you want
one-time digital delivery, no monthly software bill, and
payments tied directly to your own Stripe account.

Gumroad vs Payhip: 10% vs 5% Fees With a 2% Option

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

Gumroad and Payhip both offer ways to sell digital products,
but they take different approaches to fees, payouts, and tax
handling. For Stripe-first direct sales, Latuos is often a
simpler, lower-fee option at these price points.

Gumroad typically charges higher transaction fees on direct
sales and significantly higher fees on Discover sales. It
also operates as merchant of record, meaning it handles
taxes on behalf of sellers.

Payhip offers multiple pricing tiers, including a free plan
with higher transaction fees and paid plans with lower or no
platform fees. Latuos takes a different approach: a flat 2%
platform fee with no monthly subscription, and payouts stay
tied to the seller's own Stripe account for Stripe-first
direct sales.

Those figures compare platform fees before separate

Payhip Plus and Pro can change the math at higher volume,
but they add a monthly bill. If you only need Stripe and
want a simpler pricing model, Latuos is often lower-fee
for Stripe-first direct sales from the first sale at these
price points.

Payouts and Merchant-of-Record Tradeoffs

Payhip supports multiple payment gateways and keeps the
seller closer to the processor relationship than a
merchant-of-record platform. That can matter if the seller
wants more direct payout visibility.

Gumroad is merchant of record, which changes who controls
payout timing and parts of the refund and dispute flow.
With Gumroad, payout timing follows Gumroad's payout rules
rather than the seller's own processor account. Latuos
takes a Stripe-first approach where payouts stay tied to
the seller's own Stripe account.

Tax handling is also different. Gumroad handles tax
obligations as merchant of record, while Payhip handles
digital EU and UK VAT but may still leave other tax
obligations to the seller.

How Each Platform Takes a Different Approach

Payhip is a practical fit if you want lower-fee direct
sales than Gumroad and still want processor choice beyond
Stripe.

Gumroad is a practical fit if marketplace familiarity and
merchant-of-record convenience matter more than payout
visibility or direct-sale margins.

Latuos is often a simpler, lower-fee option for
Stripe-first direct sales at these price points when you
want no monthly subscription and payouts tied to your own
Stripe account.

Good Fit For

Sellers comparing Payhip and Gumroad because they want the
cleanest direct-sales setup with predictable fees and more
payout control.

Less Likely Fit For

Sellers who mainly want marketplace distribution or a
platform to stay fully in the middle of tax and payout
handling.

Frequently Asked Questions

Is Payhip Cheaper Than Gumroad?

Usually, yes for direct sales. Payhip Free charges 5%
plus payment processing, while Gumroad charges 10% plus
$0.50 on direct or profile sales before separate
processing fees.

How Do Payhip and Gumroad Handle Payouts Differently?

Payhip supports multiple payment gateways and keeps the
seller closer to the processor relationship. Gumroad is
merchant of record, so payout timing follows Gumroad's
payout rules instead of the seller's own processor
account.

Is Latuos Often Lower-Fee Than Both Payhip Free and
Gumroad Direct Sales?

For Stripe-first direct sales, often yes. Latuos charges
a 2% platform fee with no monthly subscription, while
Payhip Free is 5% and Gumroad direct sales are 10% plus
$0.50 before separate processing. Total costs can still
vary with payment processing and tax setup.

Does Payhip Have a Free Plan?

Yes. Payhip Free has no monthly fee and charges a 5%
transaction fee on each sale plus payment processing.
Paid plans at $29/month (Plus) and $99/month (Pro)
reduce the transaction fee to 2% and 0% respectively.

Does Gumroad Handle Sales Tax for You?

Yes. Gumroad is merchant of record and handles tax
obligations on behalf of sellers. Payhip handles digital
EU and UK VAT, but sellers may still need to manage
other taxes themselves. With Latuos, the seller is
merchant of record and manages tax through their own
Stripe account.

Can I Use My Own Domain on Payhip?

Yes. Payhip offers a custom domain feature for store
pages, while still running the hosted storefront on its
side. Gumroad also provides a hosted storefront. Latuos
provides checkout pages tied to the seller's own Stripe
account without a standalone storefront.

lower platform percentage than Payhip's Free tier charges.
The core trade-off: Latuos charges a 2% platform fee with no
monthly subscription, while Payhip Free charges 5% per
transaction before payment processing costs.

Fee Math: What You Actually Keep Per Sale

The formulas are straightforward once you separate the
platform layer from the processor layer.

Latuos formula:
Platform fee = 2% of sale price. Add Stripe processing
after that. Both deductions are visible separately in
Stripe and in Latuos analytics.

Payhip formula:
Platform fee = 5% on Free, 2% on Plus, or 0% on Pro. Add
the processing fees from whichever gateway you connect,
plus the monthly plan cost if you are on Plus or Pro.

is ahead on net platform cost.

Factors that shift the math further

Average order value.
A higher sale price increases the dollar value of each
percentage point. At $100, the 3-point gap between Latuos
and Payhip Free is $3 per transaction.

Refund and dispute rate.
Processor fees, refunds, and dispute fees follow Stripe's
current rules for your account country. Because Latuos
keeps the Stripe relationship in your account, refunds,
disputes, and fee lines are visible directly in your
Stripe dashboard.

Volume and break-even.
Payhip Plus matches Latuos's 2% platform percentage but
adds a $29 monthly cost, so it does not beat Latuos on
platform cost alone. Plus becomes cheaper than Payhip Free
once the 3-point fee saving covers $29, about $967 in
monthly gross sales, or about 39 sales at a $25 average
order.

How Payouts Work: Seller-Owned Stripe vs Platform Layer

When you connect Stripe to Latuos, the account belongs to
you. Payments from buyers flow into your Stripe balance
under your business. Latuos deducts its 2% platform fee as
part of the transaction flow, while Stripe payout
settings, dispute management, and financial reporting
remain attached to your Stripe account.

Payhip also supports connected payment gateways, including
Stripe and PayPal, so the distinction is not simply that
Payhip withholds payouts. The practical difference is that
Latuos standardizes around one Stripe-first operating
model. If payout and dispute workflow is central to your
setup, confirm the exact flow for your Payhip account type
and gateway before switching.

What to check in Stripe after connecting Latuos:

Balance and payouts: confirm your bank account and
payout schedule. Stripe says US card payments are
typically available in 2 business days, while timing can
vary by country and account history.

Revenue reporting: Latuos surfaces gross revenue, net
revenue, platform fees, and Stripe processing fees
separately.

Disputes: chargebacks appear in Stripe so you can review
evidence and respond directly through Stripe's dispute
process.

Sellers who want faster payout access can also review
Stripe Instant Payouts where available. The key point is
that these settings live in your own Stripe account when
Latuos is the platform layer.

For a deeper explanation of why account ownership matters,
see
why seller Stripe ownership matters .

Taxes and VAT: What Changes When You Switch Stacks

This is where platform models diverge sharply. Lemon
Squeezy operates as a merchant of record and includes tax
calculation and remittance in its platform model. Payhip
is different: its VAT pages say Payhip handles EU and UK
VAT for digital products as reseller, while other tax
amounts can be collected and passed to the seller for
reporting and payment.

Latuos operates on a direct seller-owned Stripe model. You
remain responsible for determining, collecting, reporting,
and remitting taxes where they apply. Switching from
Payhip to Latuos can therefore change your tax workflow if
you currently rely on Payhip's EU/UK digital VAT handling.

VAT and tax checklist for UK/EU digital product sellers:

If you are VAT-registered in the UK, confirm whether VAT
must be charged on digital goods sold to UK customers.

UK sellers selling digital goods to EU customers should
review OSS, local registration, fiscal representative,
or platform-reseller options with a qualified adviser.

If you are below the UK VAT registration threshold and
sell only to UK customers, VAT registration may not yet
apply, but confirm this against your actual business and
customer locations.

Stripe Tax is a separate Stripe product that can help
automate tax calculation and collection inside your
Stripe account. Evaluate it before choosing a direct
Stripe platform if tax automation matters.

For sellers who need platform-managed tax remittance as
part of the platform fee, compare Payhip's VAT handling
and a full merchant-of-record service such as Lemon
Squeezy before switching.

For the merchant-of-record trade-off, see the
Lemon Squeezy vs Latuos comparison .

This is general information, not tax advice. Confirm your
obligations with a qualified tax adviser for your country,
business structure, and customer locations.

Which One Is Cheaper? A Decision Guide by Selling Style

Path 1: low volume or testing

At low volume, fixed monthly costs matter most. Both
Latuos and Payhip Free cost nothing monthly, so the
difference is the platform percentage: 2% vs 5%. At this
stage Latuos is usually cheaper per sale if your buyers
can pay through Stripe.

Verdict:
Latuos or Payhip Free. Latuos is cheaper if your traffic
comes from your own channels and Stripe is enough.

Path 2: consistent sales growth

As volume grows, the per-sale percentage becomes more
significant. Latuos at 2% remains cheaper than Payhip
Free at 5%. Payhip Plus becomes relevant against Payhip
Free around $967 in monthly gross sales because the 3%
fee saving can cover the $29 monthly bill, but Plus
still costs $29 more than Latuos at the same 2% platform
percentage.

Verdict:
Latuos for sellers who want no fixed cost and direct
Stripe operations. Payhip Plus if you value Payhip's
broader product, payment, and storefront features enough
to pay the monthly bill.

Path 3: higher volume or high average order value

At higher volumes, Payhip Pro's 0% transaction fee can
win. The break-even against Latuos is $99 divided by 2%
of your average order value. At a $25 average order,
that is about 198 sales per month. At $75, it is about
66 sales per month.

Verdict:
Payhip Pro can be cheaper on platform cost once volume
is high enough. Latuos remains cleaner when you want no
monthly fee, direct Stripe reporting, and a lower cost
than Payhip Free.

In all three paths, payout control is separate from the
fee calculation. If keeping your Stripe account
independent of the platform layer matters, that factor
applies regardless of volume tier.

How to Switch from Payhip to Latuos

The migration is not technically complex, but the order of
steps matters so you avoid broken links and missed sales.

Set up Stripe if it is not already active. Verify your
identity and bank account before sending live traffic to
a new checkout.

Connect Stripe to Latuos from the dashboard so payment
processing links directly to your business account.

Upload each product: title, description, price, file, or
delivery link for templates and similar products.

Copy the new product and storefront links you will use
to replace Payhip URLs.

Test checkout and delivery using Stripe test mode or a
small live purchase you refund.

Update email sequences, social bios, link-in-bio pages,
website product pages, and any paid landing pages in
that order.

Tell your audience the product moved. Keep the message
short and focus on the new link.

Keep Payhip accessible for 30 to 60 days so previous
buyers can re-download purchases and you can access
order records.

For Notion template sellers, see the
Notion template migration guide . The storefront is different, but the delivery and link
replacement principles are the same.

Frequently Asked Questions

Is Latuos cheaper than Payhip Free?

For Stripe-first direct sales, yes in most scenarios.
Payhip Free charges 5% per transaction before processor
fees. Latuos charges 2% before Stripe processing. On a
$25 sale, that is a $0.75 platform-fee difference per
transaction. At 50 sales per month at $25, the annual
platform-fee difference is about $450 before processing
costs.

Is Payhip Pro ever cheaper than Latuos?

Yes, at sufficient volume. Payhip Pro costs $99/mo with
a 0% Payhip transaction fee. Latuos charges 2% with no
monthly fee. At a $25 average order, you need about 198
sales per month before Payhip Pro's platform-fee saving
exceeds its fixed monthly cost.

Do I still pay Stripe processing fees with Latuos?

Yes. Stripe processing fees are separate from Latuos's
2% platform fee. The example math on this page uses the
common US online card assumption of 2.9% + 30&cent;.
International cards, currency conversion, and certain
payment methods can carry different rates.

Can I sell digital downloads, templates, and eBooks?

Yes. Latuos supports downloads, templates including
Notion templates, eBooks, guides, printables, digital
art, and small bundles. For more product examples, see
digital product ideas you can sell .

What about refunds and chargebacks?

Because your Stripe account is directly connected,
refunds and dispute notifications appear in Stripe. You
can issue refunds from Stripe or from Latuos. Dispute
fees and refund fee treatment depend on Stripe's current
rules for your account country.

How do payouts work and when do I get paid?

Payouts follow the schedule in your own Stripe account.
Stripe says US card payments are typically available in
2 business days, while first payouts, risk reviews, and
country-specific rules can change timing.

Does switching from Payhip to Latuos change my VAT
obligations?

It can change your workflow. Payhip says it handles EU
and UK VAT for digital products as reseller. Latuos uses
a seller-owned Stripe model, so you remain responsible
for tax setup and compliance unless you configure a tax
tool or adviser-led process separately.

What if I need PayPal support?

Latuos is Stripe-only. If PayPal is a required payment
option for your customers, Latuos is not the right fit
at this time. Payhip offers broader payment gateway
coverage.

Is Switching Worth It?

For sellers who drive their own traffic, use Stripe, and
want to keep platform fees low without taking on a monthly
subscription, Latuos is a straightforward alternative to
Payhip Free. The 2% platform fee is lower than Payhip
Free's 5%, the Stripe relationship stays in your account,
and there is no fixed monthly cost to recover.

The case is less clear if you depend on Payhip's seller
affiliate tools, sell courses or memberships, need PayPal
support, or rely on Payhip's EU/UK VAT handling. In those
situations, the platform switch involves giving up
features or changing operations, not just changing a fee
rate.

To see the numbers for your specific price and sales
volume, use the
fee calculator .

Seller safety and platform risk guide

Is Payhip Safe? What Sellers Need to Know in 2026

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

Yes, Payhip is a legitimate platform used by many digital
sellers. The real safety question is whether you want a
platform that stays closer to your own payment processor
relationship or one that sits further in the middle of
payouts and tax handling.

If you are searching is Payhip safe or is Payhip legit, the
honest answer is that Payhip is established and usable for
many creators. The more useful seller-side question is how
much payment control, refund visibility, and dependency you
want to keep in your own stack.

Payhip is structurally closer to a processor-linked setup
compared to Gumroad because it connects sellers to supported
gateways such as Stripe, PayPal, and Paystack. Latuos is
built around a Stripe-first setup by keeping the payment
relationship tied directly to the seller's own Stripe
account.

Payhip Safe and Payhip Legit Mean Slightly Different Things

"Safe" can mean the platform is legitimate, stable enough
to launch on, and workable for taking payments. "Legit"
often means the platform is real and used by other
sellers. Those answers for Payhip are broadly yes.

The more useful seller question is structural: who owns
the payment relationship, where refunds and disputes are
easiest to see, and how much of your revenue workflow sits
inside the platform versus your own processor account.

Where Payhip Can Feel Closer to the Seller's Own Payment
Setup

Payhip connects sellers to supported processors such as
Stripe, PayPal, and Paystack. That means it is
structurally closer to the processor relationship than
Gumroad, which is merchant of record and controls payout
timing on its own side.

For many sellers, that can make Payhip feel closer to the
seller's own payment setup than a merchant-of-record
platform because refunds, disputes, and payment events are
closer to the seller's own processor stack.

Where Payhip Still Has Tradeoffs

Payhip still means you are building on top of a platform
layer. Product delivery, storefront experience, and some
account-level dependencies still sit with the platform,
even though payment handling is closer to the seller's own
processor than it is on Gumroad.

If you only need Stripe and want a more direct ownership
of the payment relationship, Latuos is built around that
kind of setup.

Who this is best for

- Sellers asking whether Payhip is legit before committing
product and payment setup time.

- Sellers comparing Payhip's processor-linked model
against Gumroad's merchant-of-record model.

- Sellers deciding whether Payhip or a Stripe-first setup
is the safer long-term fit.

Who this is not for

- Sellers who only want a yes-or-no answer about buyer
fraud protection.

- Sellers who already know they need a marketplace or a
merchant-of-record tax layer.

- Sellers looking for legal, accounting, or compliance
advice for one specific case.

Safe for What, Exactly?

Payhip can be safe enough for many digital sellers to
launch with. Buyers can pay, files can be delivered, and
the platform is established enough that the "is it legit?"
question is usually less important than the operational
setup behind the scenes.

The seller-safety question is different. Once payment
visibility, refund handling, and long-term dependency
matter more, the platform model matters more than the
buyer-facing checkout alone.

The honest answer is conditional: Payhip is a legitimate
choice, but it is still worth comparing with both Gumroad
and a Stripe-first setup like Latuos to see how much
direct control you want to keep in your own payment
account.

Refund Visibility and Processor Ownership Still Matter

Refunds and disputes reveal where the payment relationship
really lives. Payhip is closer to the seller's own
processor account than Gumroad is, and Latuos is built
directly around the seller's Stripe account from the
start. That does not make one option universally safer; it
makes the dependency tradeoff much clearer.

Stan Store pricing in 2026: plans, fees, and when the
monthly bill stops making sense

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Stan Store currently lists Creator at $29/month or
$300/year, and Creator Pro at $99/month or $948/year. Annual
billing saves $48 on Creator and $240 on Creator Pro. Stan
also says it takes 0% transaction fees, but Stripe or PayPal
processing still applies. That combination can work well for
creators who want link in bio selling tools in one place,
but the fixed subscription is harder to justify when sales
are still inconsistent.

What Stan Store pricing actually means

The headline on Stan pricing is "0% transaction fees," but
that only covers Stan's platform cut. Sellers still pay the
fixed subscription and still pay Stripe or PayPal
processing. For US Stripe transactions, Stan's help center
lists 2.9% + $0.30 as the standard purchase fee, 6.0% +
$0.30 for Afterpay, 5.99% + $0.30 for Klarna, and an extra
1.5% when the customer is in a different country.

In practice, 0% does not mean zero selling cost. It means
Stan charges a flat software subscription instead of a per
sale platform fee. If you sell through a social funnel every
month and use the built in tools heavily, that can make
sense. If you are still validating demand, the monthly bill
starts before the first sale.

Break-even lens

At 20 sales of a $25 product, Stan's $29 monthly fee works
out to $1.45 per sale before processor fees.

At the same sales volume, Latuos would take $10 in
platform fees before Stripe processing.

At 40 sales of a $25 product, Stan's fixed fee drops to
$0.73 per sale, so the economics improve as volume grows.

These are example scenarios. Actual cost depends on
product price, sales volume, and payment method fees.

Creator vs Creator Pro

Creator

Creator is $29/month or $300/year. Stan positions it as
the starter plan, and the current feature list covers a
storefront, booking tools, store analytics, course
hosting, recurring subscriptions, lead magnets, community
features, and Stan AutoDM.

This is the plan most relevant if you are comparing Stan
against Gumroad, Payhip, or a no monthly fee storefront.

Creator Pro

Creator Pro is $99/month or $948/year. It includes
everything in Creator and adds discount codes, limited
quantity offers, order bumps, payment plans, affiliate
management, email broadcasts, email flows, and pixel
tracking for Meta, Google, Pinterest, and TikTok.

That can be worthwhile if you already know you will use
the extra sales and marketing tools every month. If not,
the jump from $29 to $99 is a real pricing decision, not a
small upgrade.

When Stan Store pricing makes sense

Stan is strongest when you primarily sell through Instagram,
TikTok, or another link in bio workflow and want selling,
booking, audience capture, and follow up tools in one place.

The pricing model also gets easier to defend when monthly
revenue is predictable. Fixed software is less painful when
you already know you will use it every month.

Creator Pro makes the most sense when payment plans, order
bumps, affiliate tools, and email marketing are already part
of how you sell, not just features you might test later.

When a no-monthly-fee option is better

If you want to start at $0, test offers without a recurring
subscription, or keep checkout tied directly to your own
Stripe account, a usage-based model is usually easier to
justify.

That is where pages like

fee calculator
become more useful than Stan's headline plan grid alone.

How much does Stan Store cost in 2026?

Stan Store lists Creator at $29/month or $300/year and
Creator Pro at $99/month or $948/year. Annual billing
saves $48 on Creator and $240 on Creator Pro.

Does Stan Store include payment processing in its 0% fee
claim?

No. Stan's 0% claim refers to Stan's own platform cut.
Stripe or PayPal fees still apply, and Stan's US Stripe
fee guide lists 2.9% + $0.30 for a standard purchase, with
higher rates for Afterpay, Klarna, and some international
payments.

Does Stan Store have a free plan?

No permanent free plan is listed. Stan's official pricing
article says there is a 14 day free trial, after which you
need a paid plan.

Which Stan plan is best for beginners?

Creator is the better starting point for most beginners.
Stan positions the $29/month plan as the entry tier for
launching a link in bio store and starting sales.

Is Creator Pro worth it?

Creator Pro is easier to justify when you know you will
use payment plans, order bumps, affiliate tools, email
broadcasts, email flows, and pixel tracking. If you mainly
need a storefront and checkout, the jump from $29 to $99
can be hard to defend.

Can you sell unlimited products on Stan's $29 plan?

Stan's official pricing article says you can sell
unlimited products to unlimited customers on either paid
plan.

Is Stan Store cheaper than Gumroad?

It depends on volume. Stan has a fixed monthly
subscription with no Stan platform fee on sales, while
Gumroad has no monthly cost but charges 10% + $0.50 on
direct or profile sales and 30% on Discover sales.

Is Stan Store cheaper than Latuos?

It depends on volume and feature needs. Stan can be
cheaper at higher, predictable volume because its platform
charge is fixed, while Latuos charges 2% per sale with no
monthly fee. Latuos is usually easier to justify at low or
uneven volume.

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Stan Store uses a fixed monthly subscription with 0% Stan

Whop fees explained for digital creators

Whop Fees in 2026: Pricing, Payouts, and How Latuos Compares

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

Whop&#x27;s official materials do not frame seller costs in
one single universal way. Its current pricing pages break
charges into payments, payouts, fraud, billing, tax, and
some product-specific fees. Whop also distinguishes between
Marketplace and Whop Payments flows in its legal and
documentation pages. Latuos is narrower: direct digital
products, one visible platform fee, and payouts tied to the
seller's own Stripe account.

Based on Whop&#x27;s current public fees page reviewed in
June 2026, cards and wallets are listed at 2.7% + $0.30 for
domestic cards, plus 1.5% for international cards and 1% for
currency conversion. ACH debit is listed at 1.5% with a $5
maximum fee, and payout fees are listed separately again.

Choose Whop if you want paid groups, recurring access,
communities, or a broader app-based setup. Choose Latuos if
you want to sell direct digital products with no monthly fee
and a seller-connected Stripe flow built around simple
checkout and delivery.

Why does Whop pricing need setup-specific interpretation?

Whop&#x27;s published pricing is layered, and its official
materials currently show more than one fee frame. The
current pricing pages break costs into payments, payouts,
fraud, billing, tax, and product-specific fees, while the
legal and documentation pages distinguish between
Marketplace and Whop Payments flows. This page summarizes
those official materials reviewed in June 2026, but
sellers should still confirm which checkout and payout
flow applies to their own setup.

What fee layers does Whop publish?

Fee area

What Whop publishes

What that means

Monthly cost

Whop markets itself as free to start, with no
required monthly subscription to open an account.

The fixed monthly bill may be $0, but sellers
still need to account for variable charges tied to
payments, payouts, and optional tools.

Payment processing

Cards and wallets: 2.7% + $0.30 domestic, +1.5%
international, +1% currency conversion. ACH: 1.5%
with a $5 maximum. Financing: 15%.

Your checkout cost changes by payment rail, not
just by which platform you choose.

Payment methods and financing

Whop says it supports 100+ payment methods,
including cards, wallets, and ACH, plus financing
options for eligible businesses.

The fee picture depends partly on which payment
options you actually enable for your customers.

Payouts

Next-day ACH: $2.50. Instant bank deposit: 4% +
$1. Crypto: 5% + $1. Venmo: 5% + $1. Bank wire:
$23. International local banks: varies by country.

The effective cost of Whop can change again when
you choose how and how fast to withdraw.

Fraud and disputes

3DS: $0.03. Radar: $0.07. Dispute: $15. Early
dispute alert: $29.

These are not part of the base checkout fee, but
they can still affect real platform cost.

Billing and automation

Orchestration: 0.8%. Billing: 0.5%. Tax and
remittance: 0.5%. Affiliate processing: 1.25%.

Some Whop fees only apply when specific tools are
enabled, which makes the model harder to summarize
as one headline rate.

Platform and service fees

Whop also documents product-specific fees in some
cases. For example, its Discord app page says
adding that app to a product subjects payments on
that product to a 3% fee.

This is one reason there is no single universal
answer to "what are Whop fees?"

iOS edge case

Whop&#x27;s iOS docs say one-time purchases under
$700 in the iOS app are processed by Apple, which
charges a 30% App Store fee.

This is an app-specific exception, not the normal
web checkout flow, but it is still part of
Whop&#x27;s documented fee picture.

Pricing checked against official Whop public docs in March
2026. Processor fees, payout timing, taxes, iOS policies,
automations, and currency conversion may vary by country,
payment method, and seller setup. Latuos fee examples on
this page exclude any VAT that may apply to Latuos
platform fees for some sellers.

What is Whop actually built for?

Whop is not just a checkout button. Its pricing tools
support free, one-time, and recurring offers, and its docs
are built around products like paid groups, communities,
courses, events, software access, and other
membership-style offers.

Whop also gives sellers public store pages and the option
to show pricing links on those pages. In Whop&#x27;s paid
groups docs, it recommends choosing the right category so
people can discover the product while browsing Whop.

That matters for the comparison. If your business depends
on recurring access, community features, or a broader
app-based setup, Whop is built for that. If you mainly
want to sell direct digital goods, the extra layers may be
more than you need.

Why Whop Pricing Feels Hard to Pin Down

The main reason people struggle to answer "what are Whop
fees?" is that Whop does not present one simple seller
take rate across every setup. Its current pricing pages
break costs into payment acceptance, payouts, fraud and
dispute tooling, billing, tax automation, affiliate
processing, product-specific fees, and iOS purchases.

Whop&#x27;s legal and documentation pages also distinguish
between Marketplace sales and Whop Payments flows. That is
why sellers need to verify the exact flow attached to
their account instead of assuming one universal fee path.

The safest reading is not "Whop fee equals X," but "Whop
publishes a fee stack that changes with product type,
payment rail, payout method, enabled services, and seller
setup."

Two Example Fee Scenarios

$100 domestic card sale, next-day ACH payout

Whop&#x27;s listed domestic card fee is $3.00 on a
$100 sale. If the seller then withdraws by next-day
ACH, Whop lists another $2.50 payout fee.

Example assumes a US domestic online card payment
using the current card-pricing path on Whop&#x27;s
fees page, with no optional add-ons enabled.

Whop-listed total before optional extras: $5.50

On Latuos, a $100 sale is about $5.20 using the 2%
platform fee plus standard US Stripe domestic card
pricing. Latuos does not add a separate Latuos payout
fee.

$100 domestic card sale, instant bank payout

Whop&#x27;s listed domestic card fee is still $3.00,
but instant bank payout is listed at 4% + $1.00, which
adds another $5.00 on a $100 payout.

Example assumes a US domestic online card payment
using the current card-pricing path on Whop&#x27;s
fees page, with no optional add-ons enabled.

Whop-listed total before optional extras: $8.00

This is why Whop is not always "cheap" or "expensive"
in one universal way. The payout choice materially
changes the effective cost.

These examples are not a quote for every Whop seller. They
are simple summaries of fee components Whop publishes
publicly for the web checkout and payout paths above,
assume a US domestic online card payment, and exclude
optional fraud, billing, tax, app, and iOS-specific fees.

When is Latuos simpler than Whop for direct digital
products?

Whop can support one-time sales too, but Latuos is simpler
when the main job is selling direct digital products like
PDFs, Notion templates, design assets, printables,
tutorial files, and downloadable guides with one visible
platform fee plus standard Stripe processing.

That does not mean Whop is always more expensive on every
transaction. It means the Whop setup usually has more
moving parts. If your business depends on communities,
memberships, chat, app access, or automations, that
tradeoff may be worth it. If you want payouts tied
directly to your own Stripe account with no monthly fee
and no Latuos payout charge, Latuos is the cleaner
comparison.

Best for

Creators who want paid groups, recurring access,
community-led products, or app-based offers and are
comfortable confirming which Whop fee path applies to
their setup.

Not the best fit for

Sellers who mostly want straightforward digital products
and prefer no monthly fee, direct Stripe payouts, and
fewer moving parts around payments and withdrawals.

content:
Suspension recovery and migration guide

Gumroad Account Suspended: What to Check Next

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

If your Gumroad account is suspended, first identify the
stated reason, preserve product and sales records, contact
Gumroad support with specific evidence, and maintain
customer delivery while the review is open.

Some suspensions are temporary and reviewable. Others may be
permanent if Gumroad determines there is a policy,
prohibited-product, fraud, or payment-partner issue. The
suspension notice and Gumroad support thread are the source
of truth for your case.

Account suspension on any merchant-of-record platform can
affect more than your storefront. If the platform also
controls the payout flow, a suspension can delay payouts or
limit access to payout-related account features while the
review is open.

Why Gumroad May Suspend an Account

Gumroad's public help docs point sellers to its terms,
prohibited-product rules, fraud review, and account-review
process. Common official categories include policy
violations, products Gumroad does not allow, suspicious
purchase activity, high-risk transactions, or issues that
payment partners reject.

Forum and creator posts often mention identity checks,
VPNs, or mismatched account signals, but those should be
treated as creator reports unless Gumroad confirms them in
your own support thread. Do not assume your case is the
same as another seller's story.

Temporary vs Permanent Suspension

A temporary suspension usually means the account needs
review before normal selling or payout access returns. A
permanent suspension means Gumroad does not intend to
reopen the account under its current review outcome.

The practical difference is what you do next. For a
temporary review, focus on the appeal and requested
documentation. If Gumroad says the decision is permanent,
focus on customer communication, product access, and a
replacement checkout path.

How to Appeal or Ask for Review

Start from the reason Gumroad gives you. Reply with the
suspension notice, product URLs, product descriptions,
proof that you own or are allowed to sell the files, and
any identity or business details Gumroad asks for.

Keep the message factual. Avoid guessing that the
suspension was automatic or unfair unless Gumroad says so.
The goal is to answer the review reason clearly enough for
support to evaluate the account.

What Happens to Pending Payouts and Customers

Pending payouts may be affected while Gumroad reviews the
account. Check the balance page, payout settings, and
support notices, then keep a local record of affected
sales, refund requests, and customer-delivery status.

If buyers are waiting for files or updates, communicate
with them from a channel you still control. The page on
Gumroad payout holds
explains the payout side in more detail.

Why the Payment Layer Matters During a Suspension

Because Gumroad is merchant of record, Gumroad sits
between the customer payment and the seller. A
seller-owned Stripe setup reduces that dependency because
the payment account is not owned by the storefront
platform. Stripe can still review accounts, apply
reserves, or delay payouts under its own rules.

If you are considering a switch after a suspension, the
fee calculator shows what your per-sale cost would look
like on Latuos compared to Gumroad and other supported
platforms.

What To Rebuild First After a Suspension

After a suspension, the first priority is restoring
payment collection and delivery, not finding the perfect
all-in-one platform. That means separating the business
into layers: payment account, product files, checkout
links, and customer communication.

This is where a seller-owned payment setup has an
advantage. The less of the business that lives inside one
platform account, the faster the recovery path tends to
be.

Why This Page Points to Payout Control

Suspensions are when platform dependency becomes visible.
The fix is not only appealing the suspension. It is
reducing the amount of the business that depends on one
platform account.

Who this is best for

- Sellers dealing with a current suspension.

- Sellers with a live product business who need a recovery
and migration plan.

- Sellers deciding whether to rebuild on another bundled
platform or move to Stripe-direct infrastructure.

Who this is not for

- Sellers looking for support-policy answers only.

- Sellers with no audience or no validated product yet.

- Sellers who want a full marketplace replacement with
built-in discovery.

Rebuild order after a suspension

The first job is not redesigning the business. It is
restoring delivery for paid customers. If buyers are
waiting on files, access links, or updates, solve that
before worrying about lower-priority catalog cleanup. A
short support page, a working contact email, and a simple
temporary delivery method can reduce customer damage
quickly.

Checkout comes second. Once delivery is stable for
existing buyers, re-open a path for new sales with the
highest-selling products first. This is usually a smaller
set than the full catalog, and it is enough to get revenue
flowing again. After that, publish updated support details
and move the products that create the most repeat
questions or the largest share of revenue.

Long-tail listings come last. Rebuilding the full catalog
before restoring support and checkout often wastes time.
In practice, the recovery sequence is: stabilize customer
delivery, re-establish checkout, update contact/support
paths, move highest-selling products, then rebuild the
rest once revenue is no longer fully blocked.

Refunds and disputes during a suspension

Refunds and disputes matter during a suspension because
they reveal whether payment control and delivery control
are tied to the same provider. If the storefront, payout
flow, and customer-payment record all live inside one
platform account, a suspension can affect all three at
once. In a Stripe-direct model, the seller still has to
manage refunds and chargebacks, but the payment record
remains in the seller's own Stripe account rather than
behind the same storefront restriction.

Why was my Gumroad account suspended?

Gumroad's help docs point sellers to policy, product,
fraud, and account-review issues. The specific reason
should come from the suspension notice or Gumroad
support.

Can I appeal a Gumroad suspension?

Use Gumroad support and include the suspension notice,
product details, proof that you can sell the files, and
any identity or business information Gumroad asks for.
Outcomes depend on Gumroad's review.

What happens to pending payouts during a suspension?

A suspension can affect storefront access and pending
payouts at the same time. Check Gumroad's balance page
and support notices, then keep records of affected sales
and customer access.

How do I move my products if I am suspended on Gumroad?

Download your product files from Gumroad if you still
have access, or use your local copies. Create product
pages on a new platform, connect your Stripe account,
and update your public links.

Gumroad pricing breakdown

Gumroad Fees in 2026: What Sellers Actually Pay Per Sale

By Victor Organo, Founder of Latuos &middot; Updated June 16, 2026

Quick Answer

Gumroad charges 10% + $0.50 on direct and profile sales.
Card processing is not included in that fee. Discover
marketplace sales are 30%, which Gumroad says includes
processing. Payouts run weekly with a $100 USD standard
minimum threshold. Because Gumroad is merchant of record,
the fee comparison also includes Gumroad-controlled payout
eligibility and timing.

If you're checking current Gumroad fees for digital products
2026, Gumroad's published pricing has two tracks. Profile
and direct sales are 10% + $0.50 per transaction. Discover
marketplace sales are 30%.

The direct sales number is not the total cost. Gumroad's own
fee help says credit card processing and PayPal fees sit on
top of the 10% + $0.50. On Discover sales, Gumroad says the
30% fee includes processing.

That split matters if you already bring your own buyers. For
direct sellers, the real comparison is the headline fee, the
extra processing layer, and the fact that Gumroad is
merchant of record and controls the payout flow.

Gumroad Fee Percentage in 2026

Gumroad's fee help says sales made on Gumroad's website
are charged 10% + $0.50 before separate credit card
processing or PayPal fees. For sellers bringing their own
traffic, that means the real direct sale cost is higher
than the headline platform fee.

The fixed $0.50 matters most on lower priced products. A
$10 direct sale feels very different from a $100 direct
sale once the fixed fee and card processing are stacked on
top of each other.

Illustrative US domestic card examples

$5 direct card sale: Gumroad $1.45, Latuos $0.55

$10 direct card sale: Gumroad $2.09, Latuos $0.79

$25 direct card sale: Gumroad $4.03, Latuos $1.53

$100 direct card sale: Gumroad $13.70, Latuos $5.20

Math uses Gumroad's published direct sale fee and
Stripe's publicly listed US domestic card pricing
checked in June 2026. Actual processor fees vary by
country, payment method, and account. PayPal fees are
not included in these examples.

The fee calculator compares Gumroad's 10% + $0.50 against
Latuos's 2% platform fee plus Stripe processing at any
price point. The difference compounds once direct sales
become meaningful monthly revenue. If you already know the
direct-sale math and want the wider shortlist, use
Best Gumroad Alternatives in 2026 .

What the 30% Discover Fee Changes

Gumroad says the 30% rate applies when new customers find
and buy through its discover marketplace. That is a
different fee path from profile and direct sales.

Gumroad's fee help also says the 30% marketplace fee
includes all processing fees. So if you are looking at
Gumroad because of marketplace reach, you are not buying
the same thing as a seller who already has their own
audience.

For that reason, direct sale math and Discover math should
be judged separately. A seller with their own traffic is
usually comparing the 10% + $0.50 route plus processing,
not the 30% marketplace route.

Gumroad says Discover recommends products to new buyers
beyond a creator's own audience, but it is not automatic
for every Gumroad account. Its Discover help says sellers
need completed payout settings, at least $100 USD in
genuine sales, and risk-team verification, and that a
product also needs at least one successful sale, a
category, and ratings enabled to be listed. Gumroad also
says Discover-listed products are automatically opted into
its affiliate program.

Gumroad Payout Schedule in 2026

Gumroad says it supports direct bank deposits in local
currency for most countries and uses PayPal where bank
payouts are not available. It also says the standard
minimum payout threshold is $100 USD, with higher local
minimums in some countries.

If bank payouts are not supported in your country, Gumroad
says it uses PayPal instead. Its payout help says those
PayPal payouts are sent in USD, usually arrive within 1-3
business days, and carry a 2% processing fee.

Sellers can choose daily, weekly, monthly, or quarterly
payouts. Gumroad also says payouts include sales made
through the previous Friday, UTC, and that every sale has
a minimum 7 day holding period before it becomes eligible
for payout.

The faster options are limited. Gumroad says daily payouts
are only for eligible US sellers with qualifying bank
accounts and at least four prior payouts. It also says
instant payouts are US only and carry a 3% fee.

Gumroad also says bank payouts go to supported countries
in local currency and can take 2-7 business days to
process. If bank payouts are not available, Gumroad pays
out through PayPal in USD, usually within 1-3 business
days, with a 2% processing fee. For eligible US creators,
instant payouts are available up to $10K at a 3% fee once
a Gumroad account has completed four payouts.

On Latuos, the payment lands in the seller's connected
Stripe account and payout timing is then managed through
Stripe rather than a marketplace balance. If you want the
side-by-side decision between Gumroad's payout model and
selling direct on Stripe, use
Gumroad vs Selling Direct on Stripe .

What Gumroad's Fee Structure Leaves Out

Gumroad says it now acts as the Merchant of Record for all
sales and automatically handles sales tax collection and
remittance worldwide. Its pricing page also says creators
do not need to manage VAT, GST, or other international tax
requirements, and Gumroad says creator tax settings are
automatically disabled because Gumroad handles that
collection itself. That tax layer is part of what the fee
is paying for.

Gumroad also says creators can sell digital products,
e-books, courses, tutorials, memberships, and certain
service products. Its service-product help says calls,
commissions, and tip-style products are limited to
eligible accounts under Gumroad's current service rules.

It also applies product restrictions that do not show up
in the headline fee. Gumroad's prohibited-products page
bars categories such as illegal products or services,
securities and crypto products, credit repair, and
sexually explicit content. Its adult-content help also
says non-explicit NSFW work must be tagged and is only
shown to customers who opt in to adult content.

Gumroad pricing also covers more than a checkout fee for
an online store selling digital downloads. Gumroad's
pricing FAQ says creators can sell digital products,
e-books, courses, tutorials, and memberships, including
podcasts, online courses, or SaaS subscriptions. Gumroad
also says it handles the creator-side tax obligations tied
to its merchant-of-record role and collects the
appropriate taxes where it has those obligations.

So the fee is not only a checkout fee. It bundles tax
handling, platform rules, discovery visibility, and payout
control into one system. On Latuos, the 2% fee covers the
storefront and delivery layer while payments stay tied to
the seller's own Stripe account.

Best For

Creators selling digital products or memberships who are
comfortable with a merchant of record setup and want
Gumroad to handle tax collection and remittance.

Not For

Sellers who already bring most of their own traffic, want
lower direct sale fees, or want payments to land in their
own Stripe account instead of a platform payout system.

Why fee structure is only part of the decision

Fee structure matters, but it is only part of the decision
once refunds, disputes, and payout ownership are
considered. The platform that charges the customer first
also usually controls the payment workflow when something
goes wrong. That can matter just as much as the visible
take rate, especially for sellers whose direct sales are
now meaningful monthly income.

What Percentage Does Gumroad Take in 2026?

Gumroad's published pricing is 10% + $0.50 per
